### 16.2 Rigorous Econometric Remediation Protocols
1. **Outlier Treatment (The ₹70 Cr Tomato Cell, A01)**:
   - *Protocol*: For all parametric estimations of 2024 crop income, farmer "[Redacted Farmer Name]" (Row index 1206) must have her Tomato net income cell excluded as an unverified data-entry mutation, or the entire record excluded from parametric models ($N=1,328$). Her true Gram Panchayat is **Fakirpuri** (where median tomato net income is ₹1,300, not ₹7,500). Failing to clean this single cell inflates the 2024 mean household income by over 1,000% (from ₹56,377 to ₹576,135).
2. **Landholding Winsorization & Recoding (The 1.93B Acre Entry, A02)**:
   - *Protocol*: For the respondent household at Row index 984 (Vishunapur GP), `Owned_Land_(Acres)` must be recoded to `0.0 Acres` (since her raw text reads "5 बीघा" for Total and "5 बीघा" for Leased). Her Total Land is validly parsed as 1.0 Acre. Standard 99th-percentile Winsorization (capping farmland at 4.00 acres) must be applied prior to OLS regressions.
3. **Cross-Script Longitudinal Entity Resolution (A05)**:
   - *Protocol*: Direct string matching between FDB and 2025 Crop Data yields only 213 unique name matches. To construct a multi-year panel, researchers must: (i) Transliterate Devanagari names into Latin script (e.g. `'मीरा'` $\rightarrow$ `'Meera'`), and (ii) Execute composite string similarity matching on `[Farmer Name, Father/Husband Name, Gram Panchayat]` with a threshold $\ge 0.85$, which yields approximately 107 verified multi-year linkages.
4. **Dual-Track Productivity Reporting Standards**:
   - *Protocol*: Analysts must explicitly distinguish between **Plot-Level Intensive Yield** (agricultural income divided by dedicated vegetable plot area, which reached ₹1,31,977/acre in 2025 across 128.645 acres) and **Whole-Farm Landholding Productivity** (agricultural income divided by total operational landholding, which averaged ₹110,969/acre in 2024 and ₹19,162/acre in 2025). Conflating these two distinct metrics creates misleading evaluations of project achievements.

## Quantitative Conclusion & Complete Workspace File Traceability Crosswalk

### Quantitative Synthesis Conclusion
The recomputed quantitative microdata repository of the FASAL project demonstrates substantial agrarian progress among forest-fringe smallholders in Bahraich District, while grounding all conclusions in verified empirical realities:
1. **Agricultural Graduation**: Household net crop income experienced a **1.63-fold expansion (+63.1%)**, rising from a baseline mean of ₹34,555.01 in 2022 to ₹56,377.16 in 2024 (matching the external Midline Evaluation report of +66.7%). Programmatic target attainment (crossing ₹60,000/acre/year) reached **68.2% (CSV) or 75.0% (RAW)** under the 0.20 acre/bigha assumption, but scales down to **48.8% / 55.8%** at 0.25 acre/bigha and **5.8% / 8.1%** at 0.619 acre/bigha.
2. **Commercial Horticultural Transition**: In 2025, commercial vegetable cultivation engaged 734 active growers generating **₹1,69,78,270** across **128.645 acres** (₹1,31,977/acre), dominated by Onion (₹74.47 Lakhs) and Turmeric (₹23.16 Lakhs). An unmerged field tranche (`Sheet2`) represents an additional **373 active producers, ₹73.29 Lakhs net income, and 64.50 acres**.
3. **Nutritional Resilience**: Longitudinal pulse consumption expanded by **+11.27%** across a 5-item legume composite. Summer is confirmed as the peak of green vegetable intake (67.2% to 69.5%), inverting earlier claims of a summer hunger gap.
4. **Public Scheme Convergence**: Federation collective action mobilized **₹7,70,98,360 (~₹7.71 Crore) across 577 genuine projects** between 2023 and 2025 (PMAY Housing: ₹3.36 Cr across 280 units; Roads and Drains: ₹2.86 Cr across 60 items; Har Ghar Nal: ₹94.50 L across 665 connections). Prior claims of ₹11.70 Crore were artifacts of spreadsheet subtotal and total row double-counting.

## Part 17: Grounded Empirical Research Question Repository (All 43 RQs Across 7 Thematic Pillars) — Remediated Publication Standard

## Executive Overview & Methodological Architecture

### 1. Epistemological Framing & Grounded Empirical Scope
This repository provides an exhaustive, publication-grade portfolio of **43 deeply grounded, primary data-based research questions** designed to anchor empirical investigations in agrarian economics, ecological agriculture, smallholder commercialization, grassroots institutionalism, and survey econometrics.

Unlike speculative agendas detached from field reality, every research question formulated here is strictly anchored in the empirical microdata, administrative registers, capacity-building logs, qualitative biographical archives, and multi-year evaluation documents generated across the 5-year lifecycle (2021–2026) of the FASAL program in Mihinpurwa Block, Bahraich District, Uttar Pradesh.

#### Methodological Reality of the Survey Instrument
A foundational requirement of this remediated research agenda is an honest, scientifically accurate characterization of the primary household survey instrument:
1. **Single-Round Cross-Sectional Survey with Multi-Year Retrospective Recall:**  
   The primary household dataset (`DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, derived from KoboToolbox digital archive `Family_Development_Book_-_Dehat_Dec24_-_all_versions_-_English_en_-_2026-06-22-14-05-16.xlsx`) does **not** represent three independent longitudinal panel waves. All 1,329 household forms were submitted between **6 February 2025 and 3 November 2025** via a single enumerator account (`shwetha05`). The median interview duration was **296 minutes** (~5 hours), during which enumerators recorded three consecutive years of retrospective recall (2022, 2023, 2024) across six half-year blocks and three annual crop schedules in a single sitting. Multi-year comparisons reflect retrospective recall trajectories, subject to cognitive recall smoothing and step-change boundary effects, rather than independent repeated measurements.
2. **Complete Absence of a Comparison or Control Group:**  
   100% of the 1,329 surveyed households are FASAL program participants. No non-participant, control, or spillover households were interviewed. Consequently, causal impact estimators relying on unobserved counterfactuals—such as Difference-in-Differences (DiD), Propensity Score Matching with DiD (PSM-DiD), and Synthetic Control Methods—are structurally unfeasible with workspace data alone. Causal language must be replaced with descriptive, correlational, or non-parametric trajectory analysis, unless external counterfactual data collection is explicitly declared.
3. **Complete Absence of Demographic Covariates:**  
   The Kobo household survey and the family roster contain **zero demographic variables**: no caste/social category, respondent gender/sex, respondent age, educational attainment, marital status, or household headship. The 2025 farmer roster (`AASINFOR`, 1,358 rows) has 100% null values for `Sex`, `Age`, `Category`, and `APL/BPL`. The family member repeat roster (`family_member_repeat`, 5,796 members across 1,329 households) records only individual names and five binary institutional group membership flags. It records no ages, sexes, or relationships to household head.
4. **Resolution of Underlying Data Pipeline Defects:**  
   This agenda incorporates corrections for the systemic data-cleaning defects identified during the adversarial validation audit:
   - **SYS-1 (Repeated Block Collisions):** The raw Kobo archive contains 4,467 columns with 404 repeated question labels across survey cycles. Prior automated cleaning discarded 1,468 columns by retaining only the *last* instance of duplicated names, resulting in temporal dislocations (e.g., Jul–Dec 2024 migration data labeled as Jan–Jun 2022) and dropped crop net income columns.
   - **SYS-2 (2022 Paddy Net Income Overwrite):** In cleaned CSV files, 2022 paddy net income was erroneously overwritten with negative cost (`−cost`) across 1,136 rows (−₹85.13 Lakh aggregate), whereas the raw Kobo export records `sell − cost` totaling **+₹1,58,40,719**. Correcting this shifts true 2022 mean household crop income from ₹14,554 to **₹34,555**, revealing a genuine 1.63× expansion to ₹56,377 in 2024 (matching the external midline report's +66.7% growth from ₹32,983 to ₹54,993), rather than the fabricated 3.39× claim.
   - **SYS-4 (Undisclosed Bigha Conversion):** Farmland acreage in cleaned files was derived using a local multiplier of **0.20 acre per bigha** (5 bigha = 1 acre; 1 biswa = 0.01 acre). All per-acre metrics and attainment rates scale directly with this assumption; this agenda provides explicit sensitivity reporting across 0.20, 0.25, 0.33, and 0.619 acre/bigha conversions.
   - **Public Leverage Reconciliation:** Public scheme leverage across 2023–2025 totals **₹7.71 Crore (₹7,70,98,360) across 577 genuine projects** (₹2.63 Cr in 2023, ₹2.30 Cr in 2024, ₹2.78 Cr in 2025), correcting prior double-counting of register total/subtotal rows that inflated claims to ₹11.70 Crore.
5. **Rigorous Realignment to the 17-Page Baseline Report Reality:**  
   All baseline citations are strictly aligned to the verified 17-page baseline evaluation document (`Project_RF_Baseline_Midline_ Self assessment/Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, pp. 5–16). All citations to unverified numbered tables and pages exceeding the physical 17-page boundary of the baseline report are purged. Uncollected figures—such as chemical pesticide expenditures and commercial spraying rates—are completely eliminated.

### 3. The Strict 5-Part Specification Contract
Every research question adheres strictly to a 5-part specification contract:
- **Title**: Descriptive academic heading with unique alphanumeric identifier (e.g., `Research Question RQ-01: [Title]`).
- **Core Research Question:** Formally articulated, academically framed question defining clear empirical, structural, or methodological mechanisms.
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:** Exhaustive enumeration of exact workspace filenames, workbook sheet names, and raw database variable headers.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):** Concrete quantitative metrics, baseline/midline figures, sample sizes, and empirical parameters directly documented in primary sources, strictly aligned with the 17-page baseline report (pp. 5–16), purged of phantom tables, and fully de-identified (zero PII leaks).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:** Statistically sound and feasible analytical framework. For primary recoverable questions, fully developed descriptive, pooled, non-parametric, or hurdle specifications are provided. For questions constrained by survey design, unfeasible estimators (Mundlak, Arellano-Bond, dynamic probit, Just-Pope, Translog, synthetic control, csQCA on 4 positive cases) are eliminated and replaced with valid descriptive inquiries or explicit declarations of external data dependencies.

## Pillar 1: Agro-Ecological Transition & Agronomic Intensification

```
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| PILLAR 1 OVERVIEW: AGRO-ECOLOGICAL TRANSITION & AGRONOMIC INTENSIFICATION                                              |
| Scope: Input cost rationalization, Low-External-Input Sustainable Agriculture (LEISA) bio-formulations, Non-Pesticide  |
| Management (NPM), multi-tier spatial intensification, irrigation expansion, crop intensification, and biomass dynamics. |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

### Research Question RQ-01: Input Cost Trajectories and Cereal Margins Under Ecological Transition: Descriptive Trend Analysis and Requirements for Estimating Bio-Input Elasticities

- **Core Research Question:** What are the empirical trajectories of variable cultivation expenditure, gross revenue, and net margins in cereal production (paddy and wheat) across the 2022–2024 retrospective recall period, and what data structures are econometrically required to identify the causal elasticity of net margins with respect to on-farm biological input substitution?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, `Family_Development_Book_-_Dehat_Dec24_-_all_versions_-_English_en_-_2026-06-22-14-05-16.xlsx` (Raw Kobo export).
    - Variables: `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop- Paddy in 2022`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop-paddy in 2023`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop-paddy in 2024`, `Total net income received by the farmer (selling the crop + crop kept for consumption)-paddy in 2022` (reconstructed from raw `Income received...` minus `Total cost...`), `Total net income received by the farmer (selling the crop + crop kept for consumption)-paddy in 2023`, `Total net income received by the farmer (selling the crop + crop kept for consumption)-paddy in 2024`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop-wheat in 2022`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop-wheat in 2023`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop-wheat in 2024`, `Total net income received by the farmer...-wheat in 2022-2024`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 15–16, Section `F. Analysis of Chemical Fertilizer Use in Crop Cultivation`), `FASAL Training data.xlsx` (Sheet `Training `, row 1, 210 sessions, 13,510 attendances).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline chemical dependency: At baseline, 70.62% of farmers applied synthetic Urea to paddy and 75.83% applied Urea to wheat; 67.77% applied DAP to paddy and 73.46% applied DAP to wheat (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, pp. 15–16, Section F). Bio-fertilizer application was documented among less than 1.9% of surveyed farmers.
  - Baseline cereal economics: Paddy cultivation cost averaged ₹10,449.36/acre against a net profit of ₹2,720.30/acre; wheat cultivation cost averaged ₹22,419.35/acre against a net profit of ₹3,657.23/acre (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, pp. 15–16).
  - Retrospective recall cereal trajectory: In raw microdata, aggregate paddy net income expanded from ₹1.58 Crore (2022) to ₹1.70 Crore (2023) and ₹1.87 Crore (2024) across ~1,090 growers, while wheat net income expanded from ₹1.42 Crore (2022) to ₹1.86 Crore (2023) and ₹2.17 Crore (2024). Overall household crop income grew 1.63× from ₹34,555 (2022) to ₹56,377 (2024).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Prior proposals to estimate a Translog cost function or Mundlak correlated random effects panel model are unviable. First, the survey captured only aggregate recall cost (`Total cost incurred...`) rather than disaggregated factor prices ($P_{\mathrm{fertilizer}}, P_{\mathrm{labor}}, P_{\mathrm{seed}}$). Second, the FDB survey contains **zero household-level variables recording whether a farmer prepared or applied Jeevamrit or Ghanjeevamrit**. Training logs record aggregate session headcounts without farmer IDs. Third, regressing $\ln(\Pi_{it})$ on $\ln(C_{it})$ is mechanically circular because net income is defined by identity as gross income minus cost ($\Pi \equiv Y - C$).
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Pooled OLS descriptive regression with cluster-robust standard errors at the Gram Panchayat level:
    $$\ln(\Pi_{it} + c) = \alpha + \beta_1 \ln(C_{it}) + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \sum_{t=2023}^{2024} \tau_t D_t + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_{it}$$
    where $c$ is a hyperparameter for zero/negative net incomes, $\mathrm{Land}_i$ is operational landholding, $D_t$ are recall year fixed effects, and $\mathbf{V}_j$ are Gram Panchayat fixed effects.
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Estimating causal factor substitution and bio-input elasticity requires collecting plot-level input records tracking specific kilograms of Jeevamrit/Ghanjeevamrit applied, itemized expenditures across organic vs. chemical inputs, and sampling an untreated control group of non-adopting cereal farmers.

---

### Research Question RQ-02: Horticultural Productivity and Margins Under Botanical Pest Management: Cross-Sectional Analysis of 2025 Production and Protocol for Controlled Field Trials

- **Core Research Question:** What are the physical yield levels and net revenue margins realized by smallholders cultivating high-value horticultural crops under community-promoted Non-Pesticide Management (NPM) protocols, and what experimental protocol is required to rigorously test yield variance and cost differences against synthetic chemical insecticide regimes?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx` (Sheet `Crop details`).
    - Variables: `Bitter gourd (Karela) Area in Acre`, `Bitter gourd (Karela) Total Quantity seed used(kg)`, `Bitter gourd (Karela) Yield (Quintal)`, `Bitter gourd (Karela) Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Area in Acre`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Total Quantity seed used(kg)`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Yield (Quintal)`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Cauliflower (Phulgobi) Area in Acre`, `Cauliflower (Phulgobi) Total Quantity seed used(kg)`, `Cauliflower (Phulgobi) Yield (Quintal)`, `Cauliflower (Phulgobi) Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Bhindi Area in Acre`, `Bhindi Yield (Quintal)`, `Bhindi Total Net Income(Rs)`.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 12–14, Section `D. Cropping pattern in Kharif Rabi and zaid season`), `FASAL Training data.xlsx` (Sheet `Training `, row 6: 12 sessions on pest management/botanical sprays, 598 attendances).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline pest management reality: The 17-page baseline evaluation contains **no data on chemical pesticide expenditures or commercial spraying percentages**. Unverified assertions of commercial pesticide outlays are absent from baseline evaluation records and are purged. Baseline Section D documents high crop vulnerability to pest infestation and seasonal weather shocks without quantified chemical outlays.
  - Training exposure: Project registers log 12 training sessions dedicated to botanical decoctions (such as *Char Chatni* and *Neemastra*), recording 598 cumulative attendances (`FASAL Training data.xlsx`).
  - 2025 Horticultural production: Across 734 active producers in the 2025 schedule, vegetable growers achieved robust physical output without commercial insecticide packages: 42 Bitter Gourd growers produced 239.4 quintals on 5.28 acres, earning ₹9,57,600 net income (mean ₹22,800/grower); 75 Cauliflower growers produced 611.0 quintals on 7.37 acres, earning ₹12,22,000 net income (mean ₹16,293/grower); 40 Bhindi/Okra growers produced 142.5 quintals on 4.10 acres, earning ₹2,85,000 net income (`Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx`).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: A Just-Pope stochastic production function ($Y = f(X) + g(X)\varepsilon$) or three-stage FGLS risk model cannot be estimated because the dataset does not record input application frequencies, spray counts, or pesticide dosages. Furthermore, seed quantities are recorded as free text (e.g., "100gm", "50gm"), and there is no counterfactual chemical-using comparison group in the 2025 register.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Cross-sectional smallholder productivity frontier using parsed seed quantities:
    $$\ln(Y_{ij}) = \beta_0 + \beta_1 \ln(\mathrm{Area}_{ij}) + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{SeedQty}_{ij}^*) + \sum_{k} \delta_k \mathrm{CropDummy}_{kij} + \varepsilon_{ij}$$
    where $Y_{ij}$ is physical yield in quintals for crop $j$, $\mathrm{Area}_{ij}$ is plot acreage, and $\mathrm{SeedQty}_{ij}^*$ is seed quantity parsed to kilograms.
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Identifying the causal impact of NPM on yield variability and pesticide expenditure requires split-plot field trials or paired observational cohorts logging weekly pest counts, spray volumes, active ingredient costs, and crop damage across NPM versus conventional chemical plots.

---

### Research Question RQ-03: Multi-Tier Trellis Architecture (*Machan*): Observational Status in Workspace Data and Empirical Design for Spatial Agro-Ecological Intensification

- **Core Research Question:** What empirical evidence of vertical multi-tier trellis (*Machan*) cultivation exists within project records, and how can a spatial Land Equivalent Ratio (LER) framework be formally designed to evaluate multi-canopy smallholder farming?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx` (Sheet `Crop details`).
    - Variables: `Jall Taar Area in Acre`, `Jall Taar Total  Jaal Taar`, `Tomato (Trellis) Area in Acre`, `Tomato (Trellis) Yield (Quintal)`, `Tomato (Trellis) Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Bitter gourd (Karela) Area in Acre`, `Bitter gourd (Karela) Yield (Quintal)`, `Bitter gourd (Karela) Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Area in Acre`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Yield (Quintal)`, `Ridge gourd (Taroyi) Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Turmeric Area in Acre`, `Turmeric Yield (Quintal)`, `Turmeric Total Net Income(Rs)`.
  - Secondary Files: `FASAL_2025/Case stories/केस स्टडी राजकुमारी जी_Final.docx`, `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Structural data absence in primary register: In `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx`, the dedicated columns for trellis systems—`Jall Taar Area in Acre` and `Tomato (Trellis) Area in Acre`—contain **exactly 0 non-zero entries (0 growers and 0 acres recorded)**. Trellis farming was promoted as an agronomic method and appears in field photo archives, but was not captured as an independent accounting category in the 2025 crop schedule.
  - Vegetable and rhizome yields: Farmers cultivated the crops suited for trellis canopies and ground shading as separate entries: 42 bitter gourd growers (5.28 ac, 239.4 q, ₹9.58 Lakh net); 49 ridge gourd growers (4.21 ac, 207.5 q, ₹8.30 Lakh net); and 112 turmeric growers producing **1,279.0 quintals** (not 463.2 q) across 6.91 physical acres, generating ₹23,16,000 net profit.
  - Purge of ungrounded temperature claims: Claims of a "3°C to 5°C micro-climate canopy cooling effect" originated in intermediate AI drafting files and do not exist in project agro-meteorological logs.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Feasibility: Estimating Land Equivalent Ratios (LER) or a Stochastic Production Frontier on trellis adoption is impossible with workspace data because the trellis dummy variable has zero variance ($\mathrm{Var}(\mathrm{Trellis}) = 0$).
  - Proposed Empirical Protocol & External Data Requirements: To test vertical spatial efficiency, future research must implement an experimental or quasi-experimental field protocol measuring:
    $$\mathrm{LER} = \frac{Y_{\mathrm{upper}}^{\mathrm{inter}}}{Y_{\mathrm{upper}}^{\mathrm{mono}}} + \frac{Y_{\mathrm{ground}}^{\mathrm{inter}}}{Y_{\mathrm{ground}}^{\mathrm{mono}}}$$
    where $Y_{\mathrm{upper}}$ represents vertical cucurbit yield (bitter gourd, ridge gourd) and $Y_{\mathrm{ground}}$ represents shade-tolerant rhizome yield (turmeric, ginger), benchmarked against dedicated monoculture control plots on equivalent soil classes. Micro-climate buffering must be verified via in-situ soil and canopy temperature sensors.

---

### Research Question RQ-04: Pre-Monsoon Zaid Cropping Expansion and Irrigation Access: A Recall Event-Study Analysis of Subsidized Borewell Utilization

- **Core Research Question:** Did the progressive acquisition of subsidized diesel/engine irrigation borewells alter household propensities to cultivate pre-monsoon Zaid crops (okra, cowpea, cucumber), and what was the observed expansion in pre-monsoon crop income across recall half-years?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, `Family_Development_Book_-_Dehat_Dec24_...xlsx`.
    - Variables: `Did you Received engine boring in First six Months`, `...Second six Months`, `...Third six Months`, `...Fourth six Months`, `...Fifth six Months`, `...Sixth six Months`, `Total net income received by the farmer (selling the crop + crop kept for consumption)-okra in 2022`, `...okra in 2023`, `...okra in 2024`, `...cowpea in 2022-2024`, `...moong in 2022-2024`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`.
  - Secondary Files: `Jan Suvidha kendra Data 2022,23,24 -25.xlsx` (Sheet `boring avedan 2024`), `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (p. 11, Section `C. Agriculture / Source of irrigation`; p. 13, Section `D. Cropping pattern in Kharif Rabi and zaid season`).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline irrigation and fallow baseline: At baseline, 65.29% of farmers (982 of 1,504) depended on rented diesel engine pumps, while 33.98% (511 farmers) had no irrigation access (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, p. 11, Section C). Section D (p. 13) confirms that due to dry-season water constraints, three-fourths of cultivable land remained fallow during the pre-monsoon Zaid season.
  - Subsidized borewell demand: The JSK registry logs 352 subsidized borewell applications submitted in 2024 (`boring avedan 2024`), with Karikot (118), Chahalwa (102), and Badkhadiya (58) representing 78.9% of demand. Note: this sheet records applications (*avedan*), not installation dates or operating status.
  - FDB boring receipt and Zaid expansion: In the FDB retrospective recall blocks, households reporting receipt of an engine boring expanded from 225 (first half-year) to 273 (sixth half-year). Concurrently, active Zaid okra growers expanded from 616 to 968 households, cowpea growers from 243 to 479 households, and cucumber from 48 to 142 households (`DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: PSM-DiD is eliminated because: (1) no unassisted comparison group was surveyed; (2) the JSK boring application sheet contains no household IDs or sanction dates to merge with FDB microdata; and (3) GPS coordinates were never collected.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: A staggered recall event-study framework using the self-reported half-yearly FDB boring receipt flags:
    $$Y_{it} = \alpha_i + \sum_{k=-K}^{L} \theta_k \cdot \mathbb{I}(t - E_i = k) + \mathbf{X}_{it}'\boldsymbol{\beta} + \tau_t + \varepsilon_{it}$$
    where $Y_{it}$ is Zaid crop net income or an indicator for cultivating pre-monsoon vegetables in half-year $t \in \{1, \dots, 6\}$; $E_i$ is the recall half-year in which household $i$ first reports receiving engine boring assistance; $\alpha_i$ is a household fixed effect; $\tau_t$ is a half-year period fixed effect; and standard errors are clustered at the Gram Panchayat level.
  - Limitations: The event study relies on retrospective timing recall, which may be subject to recall bias. Identifying true water-table access and operating costs requires external post-installation surveys.

---

### Research Question RQ-05: Crop Intensification Systems (CIS) in Cereal Cultivation: Descriptive Output Comparisons and Agronomic Trial Requirements

- **Core Research Question:** How do cereal yields and input costs compare across smallholders in villages with high versus low exposure to Crop Intensification System (CIS) line-sowing demonstrations, and what agronomic data is necessary to evaluate the technical efficiency of CIS?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, `Family_Development_Book_-_Dehat_Dec24_...xlsx`.
    - Variables: `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop- Paddy in 2022-2024`, `Total net income received...-paddy in 2022-2024`, `Total cost...-wheat in 2022-2024`, `Total net income...-wheat in 2022-2024`, `Village Panchayat`, coalesced raw `Village Name` (7 columns).
  - Secondary Files: `Grant Application APPI_DEHAT V2.0 31st Aug.docx` (CIS line sowing specifications, 25–30 cm spacing), `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 12–14, Section D).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline cereal yields: Baseline paddy net profit averaged only ₹2,720.30/acre with costs of ₹10,449.36/acre; wheat net profit was ₹3,657.23/acre with costs of ₹22,419.35/acre (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, pp. 15–16).
  - CIS programmatic promotion: The Grant Application specified CIS line-sowing protocols with 10–12 inch (25–30 cm) grid spacing. Note: mechanical cono-weeders, SRI/SWI tiller counts (25–35 tillers), and seed rate drops from 40 to 18 kg/acre are unrecorded in project monitoring files and are purged.
  - Microdata coverage: In the raw Kobo export, paddy net income grew from ₹1.58 Crore (2022) to ₹1.87 Crore (2024). In the cleaned CSV, village-level analysis was blocked because 93.4% of rows were null due to un-coalesced cascade columns (SYS-1); coalescing the 7 raw village columns restores 100% village assignment across the 18 distinct villages.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Panel fixed effects with instrumental variable line-sowing adoption cannot be estimated because the FDB survey contains **no variable recording whether a household adopted CIS line-sowing or broadcast sowing**.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive cross-village comparison of cereal revenue-to-cost ratios ($\Pi / C$) across villages categorized by programmatic exposure derived from monthly review decks, using raw-coalesced village indicators:
    $$\frac{\Pi_{it}^{\mathrm{cereal}}}{C_{it}^{\mathrm{cereal}}} = \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{HighExposureVillage}_j + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \tau_t + \varepsilon_{ijt}$$
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Estimating the agronomic and economic returns of CIS requires on-farm trial plots comparing line-sown versus broadcast plots on the same holding, measuring seeding rates, labor hours for weeding, effective tiller counts, and harvest weights.

---

### Research Question RQ-06: Bund Agroforestry, Bovine Assets, and Variable Crop Cost Dynamics: A Hurdle and Correlational SUR Model

- **Core Research Question:** Is household ownership of bovine livestock (cows and buffaloes) associated with lower variable crop cultivation expenditures, and to what extent does commercial sale of livestock by-products supplement smallholder crop revenue?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, `Family_Development_Book_-_Dehat_Dec24_...xlsx`.
    - Variables: `Do you have Cow in first six month`, `...Second six Months`, `...Third six Months`, `...Fourth six Months`, `...Fifth six Months`, `...Sixth six Months`, `Do you have Buffalo in first six month` (through Sixth six Months), `Income received by the farmer from selling livestock by product-Cow`, `Income received by the farmer from selling livestock by product-Buffalo`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop- Paddy in 2024`, `Total cost incurred by the farmer in growing the crop-wheat in 2024`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (p. 6, Section A; pp. 15–16, Section F), `Grant Application APPI_DEHAT V2.0 31st Aug.docx` (p. 14: Bund plantation design).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Bovine ownership reality: In the FDB microdata, **224 households (16.85%)** report owning any bovine livestock (cows or buffaloes) across recall periods. Prior claims that ">74% own livestock" are contradicted by the microdata.
  - Binary livestock variables: The survey recorded livestock ownership strictly as binary Yes/No flags (`Do you have Cow...`), not animal headcounts.
  - Livestock by-product revenue: Commercial livestock by-product sales are highly sparse: only 15 cow-owning households (mean ₹8,467, max ₹35,000) and 47 buffalo-owning households (mean ₹11,362, max ₹70,000) report positive commercial by-product income.
  - Bund agroforestry data absence: Field bund tree planting (*Gliricidia*, *Acacia*) was proposed in the project grant application, but no seedling distribution records or dung-fuel substitution surveys were ever administered. Claims that "100% of dung was liberated from cooking" are ungrounded.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model (Primary Recoverable Specification):**
  - Model Formulation: A two-part Hurdle and Seemingly Unrelated Regression (SUR) system modeling crop cultivation costs conditioned on bovine ownership, paired with a selection model for commercial by-product revenue:
    $$\begin{aligned}
    C_{i}^{\mathrm{paddy}} &= \alpha_1 + \gamma_1 \mathrm{Bovine}_i + \beta_1 \mathrm{Land}_i + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\delta}_1 + u_{1i} \\
    C_{i}^{\mathrm{wheat}} &= \alpha_2 + \gamma_2 \mathrm{Bovine}_i + \beta_2 \mathrm{Land}_i + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\delta}_2 + u_{2i}
    \end{aligned}$$
    where $\mathrm{Bovine}_i = \mathbb{I}(\mathrm{Cow}_i = 1 \lor \mathrm{Buffalo}_i = 1)$, $C_i$ denotes total variable crop costs in 2024, $\mathbf{V}_j$ are village fixed effects, and error terms $(u_{1i}, u_{2i})$ are allowed to correlate ($\mathrm{Cov}(u_1, u_2) \ne 0$).
  - Second-Stage By-Product Revenue Hurdle:
    $$\Pr(R_i^{\mathrm{byprod}} > 0) = \Phi(\mathbf{Z}_i'\boldsymbol{\theta})$$
    $$\ln(R_i^{\mathrm{byprod}} \mid R_i^{\mathrm{byprod}} > 0) = \mathbf{Z}_i'\boldsymbol{\psi} + \sigma \lambda(\mathbf{Z}_i'\boldsymbol{\theta}) + \nu_i$$
    testing whether bovine ownership significantly lowers variable input purchases ($\gamma_1 < 0, \gamma_2 < 0$) while identifying the determinants of market participation in dairy/manure sales.
