### Research Question RQ-07: Testing the Inverse Farm Size-Productivity Hypothesis: Non-Parametric Kernel Smoothing and Bigha Conversion Sensitivity Analysis

- **Core Research Question:** Does an inverse relationship between operational farm size and net crop income per acre hold across the 1,160 surveyed smallholders, and how sensitive is the empirical elasticity to alternative local bigha-to-acre conversion factors and land-accounting math violations?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Total_Land_(Acres)`, `Owned_Land_(Acres)`, `Leased_Land_(Acres)`, `Total net income received by the farmer (selling the crop + crop kept for consumption)-paddy in 2024`, `...wheat in 2024`, `...okra in 2024`, `...mustard in 2024`, `...potato in 2024`, `Village Panchayat`.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 9–10, Section `C. Agriculture / Availability of Land`), `Family Development Booklet-DEHAT.pdf` (Questionnaire land items).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline land distribution: At baseline, 71.08% of farmers cultivated land sizes ranging from 0.1 to 5.1 bighas, with the baseline report explicitly declaring: *"71.08% of farmers cultivate land sizes ranging from 0.1 to 5.1 bighas, (5 bighas equal to 1 acre)"* (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, pp. 9–10, Section C). 88.5% were marginal farmers (<1 hectare).
  - Microdata sample parameters: Valid landholding data is recorded for 1,160 households (169 missing), aggregating to 886.04 acres at 0.20 acre/bigha (mean 0.764 ac, median 0.50 ac, p99 4.00 ac).
  - Verified size-class distribution:
    - $\le 0.50$ acre: 558 households (51.2% of non-zero landholders), holding 164.16 acres (mean 0.294 ac).
    - $>0.50$ to $1.00$ acre: 372 households (32.1%), holding 291.98 acres (mean 0.785 ac).
    - $>1.00$ to $2.00$ acres: 127 households (10.9%), holding 206.50 acres (mean 1.626 ac).
    - $>2.00$ to $4.00$ acres: 56 households (4.8%), holding 164.20 acres (mean 2.932 ac).
    - $>4.00$ to $8.00$ acres: 11 households (0.9%), holding 59.20 acres (mean 5.382 ac).
    - Total Land = 0.00: 36 households (3.1% of reporting sample).
  - Observed inverse productivity pattern: In 2024, mean net crop income per acre was **₹1,13,907/acre** for farms $\le 0.50$ acre, compared to **₹82,414/acre** for 0.50–1.00 acre, **₹64,120/acre** for 1.00–2.00 acres, and **₹54,846/acre** for farms $>2.00$ acres. Across all 1,124 non-zero landholders, mean per-acre return was ₹72,724 (median ₹58,000).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model (PRIMARY RECOVERABLE RQ):**
  - Data Hygiene Protocol: Filter out the 176 households violating the internal accounting identity $\mathrm{Owned} + \mathrm{Leased} = \mathrm{Total}$, as well as Row 984 (where an enumerator phone number was entered into bigha), leaving $N=966$ geometrically consistent landholding households.
  - Non-Parametric Kernel-Weighted Local Polynomial Regression:
    $$\frac{\Pi_i}{A_i} = m(\ln A_i) + \varepsilon_i$$
    estimated via Epanechnikov kernel smoothing with bandwidth selected by leave-one-out cross-validation:
    $$\hat{m}(a) = \frac{\sum_{i=1}^{N} K\left(\frac{\ln A_i - a}{h}\right) \frac{\Pi_i}{A_i}}{\sum_{i=1}^{N} K\left(\frac{\ln A_i - a}{h}\right)}$$
  - Semi-Parametric Partially Linear Model:
    $$\frac{\Pi_i}{A_i} = g(\ln A_i) + \mathbf{X}_i'\boldsymbol{\beta} + \varepsilon_i$$
    where $g(\cdot)$ is an unknown smooth function and $\mathbf{X}_i$ includes village indicators and crop diversity counts.
  - Parametric Double-Log Benchmark:
    $$\ln\left(\frac{\Pi_i}{A_i}\right) = \alpha + \beta_1 \ln(A_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i$$
    Testing $H_0: \beta_1 = 0$ against $H_1: \beta_1 < 0$ (verifying the inverse productivity hypothesis).
  - Explicit Bigha Sensitivity Matrix: Re-estimate $\beta_1$ across four conversion factors:
    $$\text{Conversion Factors: } \kappa \in \{0.20 \text{ (local baseline)}, 0.25 \text{ (4 bigha/ac)}, 0.333 \text{ (3 bigha/ac)}, 0.619 \text{ (standard UP pacca bigha)}\}$$
    demonstrating that while absolute per-acre returns scale by $1/\kappa$, the elasticity $\beta_1$ remains invariant under linear scalar transformations but shifts under non-linear biswa rounding.

---

### Research Question RQ-08: Smallholder Graduation Dynamics: Markov Transition Matrices and Descriptive Analysis of the ₹60,000/Acre Benchmark

- **Core Research Question:** What are the empirical transition probabilities of households crossing and maintaining the ₹60,000/acre net farm income threshold across recall years, and what household-level factors correlate with sustained attainment?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, Raw Kobo Export.
    - Variables: `Total_Land_(Acres)`, Corrected Total Net Crop Income (2022, 2023, 2024), `Total net income...-paddy in 2022-2024`, `...wheat in 2022-2024`, `...okra in 2022-2024`.
  - Secondary Files: `RF Template (5year) Ver.2.0 16th Aug.xlsx` (Outcome Indicator 1.1: ₹60,000/acre target).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Attainment recomputation: Correcting the 2022 paddy net income overwrite (SYS-2) substantially revises baseline attainment:
    - 2022: 346 of 1,123 non-zero landholders (30.8%) achieved $\ge ₹60,000/\mathrm{acre}$ in raw data (87 households / 7.8% in uncorrected CSV; 296 / 26.4% in corrected CSV).
    - 2023: 638 households (56.9% in raw; 560 / 49.9% in CSV).
    - 2024: 842 households (75.0% in raw; 765 / 68.1% in CSV) achieved $\ge ₹60,000/\mathrm{acre}$ at 0.20 acre/bigha.
  - Multi-bigha sensitivity at 2024 endline: Attainment is highly sensitive to the bigha assumption: **68.1% at 0.20 ac/bigha**, falling to **48.8% at 0.25 ac/bigha**, **24.9% at 0.333 ac/bigha**, and **5.8% at 0.619 ac/bigha**.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Dynamic panel probit with Wooldridge initial conditions is eliminated. With $T=3$ recall periods collected in a single sitting, true structural state dependence ($\Pr(y_{it}=1 \mid y_{i,t-1}=1)$) cannot be separated from retrospective recall smoothing and persistent unobserved heterogeneity.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: First-order empirical Markov transition probability matrix:
    $$\mathbf{P} = \begin{pmatrix} p_{00} & p_{01} \\ p_{10} & p_{11} \end{pmatrix}$$
    where $p_{jk} = \Pr(S_{it} = k \mid S_{i,t-1} = j)$ for status $S_{it} = \mathbb{I}(\Pi_{it}/A_i \ge ₹60,000)$.
  - Correlated Random-Effects Probit (Descriptive Benchmark):
    $$\Pr(S_{it} = 1 \mid \mathbf{Z}_{it}) = \Phi(\alpha + \rho S_{i,t-1} + \mathbf{X}_{it}'\boldsymbol{\beta} + \bar{\mathbf{X}}_i'\boldsymbol{\lambda} + \alpha_i)$$
    interpreted strictly as descriptive conditional mobility rather than causal state dependence.

---

### Research Question RQ-09: Land Tenure Typologies and Crop Income Margins: Methodological Artifacts and Descriptive Comparisons Across Tenurial Classes

- **Core Research Question:** How do net crop returns per acre compare across pure owner-operators, pure tenants, and mixed owner-tenant smallholders, and to what extent are observed tenure categories distorted by survey data-entry artifacts?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Owned_Land_(Acres)`, `Leased_Land_(Acres)`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`, `How much Owned Agriculture land is available with you?(in bigha)`, `How much leased agriculare land is available with you?(in bigha)`, Total Net Crop Income 2024.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 9–10, Section `C. Agriculture / Availability of Land`).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline tenure: Baseline report Section C (pp. 9–10) documents that 8% of farmers were landless lessees and 22% engaged in leasing-in land under informal sharecropping (*batai*).
  - Microdata tenure distribution and data-entry artifacts:
    - Pure owners: 954 households (82.2% of 1,160), holding 750.07 Total acres (mean 0.786 ac) and 757.45 Owned acres (prior claim of 634.58 ac was an arithmetic error).
    - Mixed owner-tenants: 160 households (excluding phone row). However, **137 of these 160 households (85.6%) have identical figures entered into owned, leased, and total fields** (e.g., "2 bigha" entered into all three boxes), creating spurious leasing numbers.
    - Pure tenants: Exactly 24 households (2.1%), cultivating 16.02 total acres (mean 0.668 ac, range 0.02 to 2.00 ac).
    - Zero both: 21 households report 0 in both sub-fields.
  - Tenure productivity: In 2024, pure owners achieved mean per-acre return of ₹71,940/ac; pure tenants achieved ₹78,320/ac; mixed tenants achieved ₹75,110/ac (at 0.20 ac/bigha).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Endogenous Switching Regression Models (ESRM) cannot be estimated because: (1) tenure typology is corrupted by the 137 duplicate entry rows; (2) plot-level tenure assignments do not exist; and (3) no valid instrumental variable predicting tenure choice is present in workspace files.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive OLS with tenure class indicators, restricting the sample to validated non-duplicate tenure records ($N=1,010$):
    $$\frac{\Pi_i}{A_i} = \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{PureTenant}_i + \beta_2 \mathrm{MixedTenureValidated}_i + \beta_3 \ln(A_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i$$
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Investigating tenurial disincentives (Marshallian inefficiency) requires plot-level tenancy contracts, formal rental/sharecropping terms (50:50 cost/output sharing), and parcel-specific input logs.

---

### Research Question RQ-10: Deconstructing the "Landless Cultivator" Cohort: Item Non-Response Analysis and Unrecorded Horticultural Micro-Plots

- **Core Research Question:** Does the subset of households reporting zero or missing landholdings whilst recording positive crop revenue represent true landless cultivation or survey item non-response, and what are the characteristics of unrecorded micro-plots in horticultural registers?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx`.
    - Variables: `Total_Land_(Acres)`, `_uuid`, `_index`, 2024 Crop Net Incomes, 2025 Crop Areas and Net Incomes.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - FDB Landless Breakdown: Among the 163 households reporting positive 2024 crop income without recorded land:
    - **129 households have MISSING land data (`Total_Land_(Acres)` is null)**. Their crop incomes range from ₹1,200 to ₹2,08,000, indicating that enumerators skipped the landholding question rather than that the farmers were landless.
    - **34 households have recorded land of exactly 0.00 acres**. Their crop incomes range from ₹8 to ₹65,000 (median ₹14,200).
  - 2025 Register Zero-Area Phenomenon: In the 2025 vegetable schedule, 383 crop entries record 0.00 acres but positive income. These represent unrecorded homestead micro-plots (e.g., methi recorded as 0.00 acres for 287 growers with positive yields; onion micro-beds). Fictitious sociological claims of *nautor* or *bari* cultivation are unsupported by project documentation.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Zero-Inflated Poisson (ZIP) and Type-II Tobit selection models are inappropriate because the missingness is an administrative survey artifact rather than an economic selection process.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Missing-data mechanism diagnostics (Little's MCAR test) and binary logistic missingness regression:
    $$\Pr(\mathrm{LandMissing}_i = 1) = \Lambda(\alpha + \mathbf{Y}_i^{\mathrm{crops}\prime}\boldsymbol{\beta} + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \text{Enumerator}_k)$$
    testing whether land missingness correlates with specific crops or enumerator identifiers.

---

### Research Question RQ-11: High-Performing Smallholder Benchmarking: Latent Mobility and Characteristics of Sustained Top-Quartile Producers

- **Core Research Question:** What are the structural and agronomic characteristics that distinguish smallholders who consistently achieve top-quartile per-acre net returns across all three recall years, and how do corrected microdata benchmarks alter high-performer identification?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, Raw Kobo Export.
    - Variables: `Name of the Farmers/AAS Members`, `Village Panchayat`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`, Per-Acre Net Incomes (2022, 2023, 2024).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Purge of "Elite 108": The claim of "108 Elite Farmers" was an artifact of merging distinct households sharing identical name and father's name strings (SYS-6). In the uncorrected CSV, exactly **81 unique households** attained $\ge ₹60,000/\mathrm{acre}$ in all 3 years. In raw data with corrected 2022 paddy income, **319 households** (266 strictly complete cases) achieved the threshold in all 3 years.
  - De-identified profiles and corrected locations: High-performing smallholders identified in project profiles (e.g., Reeta Devi, Dongli) reside in **Fakirpuri GP**, not Karikot. Their incomes were driven by cereal, spice, and vegetable intensification on micro-holdings (0.10–0.12 ac), not by trellis systems.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Formal Latent Class Trajectory Analysis (LCTA) and Data Envelopment Analysis (DEA) Super-Efficiency are eliminated due to lack of input quantities and single-round recall limitations.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Quantile transition analysis and robust logistic benchmarking:
    $$\Pr(\mathrm{TopQuartileAllYears}_i = 1) = \Lambda(\alpha + \beta_1 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \beta_2 \mathrm{CropDiversity}_i + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma})$$
    evaluating the partial correlation between crop diversification, operational scale, and persistent high productivity.

---

### Research Question RQ-12: Smallholder Commercialization in Turmeric (*Curcuma longa*): Production Function Estimation and Value Chain Scaling

- **Core Research Question:** What are the output elasticities of land area and seed input in smallholder commercial turmeric production, and what net economic margins are realized under community-aggregated spice marketing?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx` (Sheet `Crop details`).
    - Variables: `Turmeric Area in Acre`, `Turmeric Total Quantity seed used(kg)`, `Turmeric Yield (Quintal)`, `Turmeric Total Net Income(Rs)`.
  - Secondary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv` (Variables: `Total net income...-turmeric in 2022-2024`, `Total cost...-turmeric in 2022-2024`), `Grant Report January to December 2024.pdf` (p. 18: Turmeric collective marketing).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - FDB longitudinal adoption: Turmeric cultivation expanded rapidly in FDB recall records: from 135 growers (2022) to 216 (2023) and **351 growers (2024)**, with aggregate net income rising from ₹8.84 Lakh to ₹29.98 Lakh (`DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`).
  - 2025 production schedule: In the 2025 audited schedule, **112 smallholders** cultivated turmeric across **6.91 physical acres**, producing **1,279.0 quintals** (mean yield 185.1 q/acre) and generating **₹23,16,000 net income** (mean ₹20,679 per grower; ₹3,35,166 per physical acre equivalent). Note: prior synthesis reported an erroneous yield of 463.2 quintals; the true verified sum is 1,279.0 quintals.
  - Seed inputs: Seed usage was recorded in text strings across 112 farmers (e.g., "50 kg", "100 kg", "1 quintal").
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model (PRIMARY RECOVERABLE RQ):**
  - Data Preparation: Algorithmic parsing and standardization of free-text seed entries into numeric kilograms ($\mathrm{Seed}_i$).
  - Cobb-Douglas Production Function Specification:
    $$\ln(Y_i) = \beta_0 + \beta_1 \ln(\mathrm{Area}_i) + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Seed}_i) + \varepsilon_i$$
    where $Y_i$ is physical yield in quintals (`Turmeric Yield (Quintal)`), $\mathrm{Area}_i$ is `Turmeric Area in Acre`, and $\mathrm{Seed}_i$ is parsed seed input in kg.
  - Returns to Scale Test: Test linear homogeneity:
    $$H_0: \beta_1 + \beta_2 = 1 \quad \text{vs.} \quad H_1: \beta_1 + \beta_2 \ne 1$$
    evaluating whether smallholder spice plots exhibit constant, increasing, or decreasing returns to scale.
  - FDB Longitudinal Cost-Return Model ($N=351$ in 2024):
    $$\ln(\Pi_{it}) = \alpha + \beta_1 \ln(C_{it}) + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{TotalLand}_i) + \tau_t + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_{it}$$
  - Note on Marketing Channels: The microdata does not record individual buyer types, transaction prices, or FPO sales receipts. Hedonic price models are dropped; value-chain margin analysis requires external trader transaction records.

---

### Research Question RQ-13: Crop Portfolio Specialization and Income Concentration: Decomposing Revenue Vulnerability in Commercial Horticulture

- **Core Research Question:** What is the degree of income concentration in smallholder commercial vegetable production, and how sensitive is aggregate horticultural revenue to price and yield volatility in the dominant cash crop (onion)?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx` (Sheet `Crop details`).
    - Variables: `Area in Acre`, `Total Net Income(Rs)`, `Onion Area in Acre`, `Onion Yield (Quintal)`, `Onion Total Net Income(Rs)`, and crop-specific area/income variables across all 26 crops.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - 2025 horticultural aggregate: Across 734 active vegetable producers, total net income reached **₹1,69,78,270** across **128.645 physical cultivated acres** (mean ₹23,131 per farmer; ₹1,31,978 per vegetable acre).
  - Extreme onion concentration: **413 farmers** cultivated onion across 48.74 acres, generating **₹74,47,400 net income**. Onion alone accounts for **43.86% of total horticultural net revenue** and 37.9% of total vegetable acreage.
  - Secondary commercial crops: Turmeric contributed ₹23.16 Lakh (13.64%), Cauliflower ₹12.22 Lakh (7.20%), Bitter Gourd ₹9.58 Lakh (5.64%), and Ridge Gourd ₹8.30 Lakh (4.89%). The top 5 crops generate 75.2% of total vegetable revenue.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Markowitz mean-variance portfolio optimization and Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR) models are eliminated because they require multi-year panel time series of plot-level prices and yields, whereas the crop schedule represents a single season (2025).
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Household-level Herfindahl-Hirschman Concentration Index (HHI):
    $$\mathrm{HHI}_i = \sum_{k=1}^{K_i} s_{ik}^2, \quad \text{where } s_{ik} = \frac{\Pi_{ik}}{\sum_{m} \Pi_{im}}$$
    paired with a linear revenue sensitivity simulation:
    $$\Delta \Pi_i^{\mathrm{total}} = s_{i, \mathrm{onion}} \cdot \Delta p_{\mathrm{onion}}$$
    measuring the distribution of household revenue shocks under simulated market price collapses ($\Delta p \in \{-10\%, -25\%, -50\%\}$).

---

## Pillar 3: Nutritional Security, Dietary Diversity & Seasonality

```
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| PILLAR 3 OVERVIEW: NUTRITIONAL SECURITY, DIETARY DIVERSITY & SEASONALITY                                              |
| Scope: 36-month recall food patterns, tricolor food item diversity, seasonal green vegetable peaks, legume/pulse      |
| consumption, own-farm crop retention, Bal Sansad child dietary patterns, and widow-headed household entitlements.     |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

### Research Question RQ-14: Dynamics of Reported Tricolor Food Diversity Across 36 Recall Months: Item Count Trajectories and Retrospective Recall Ceiling Constraints

- **Core Research Question:** What are the longitudinal monthly trajectories of reported household consumption across white, green, and yellow food groups over the 36-month retrospective recall window (2022–2024), and how do survey ceiling effects constrain dietary modeling?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv` (1,260 monthly food tracking columns).
    - Variables: `What are white food did you consumed in <month><year>/<item>`, `What are Green food did you consumed in <month><year>/<item>`, `What are yellow food did you consumed in <month><year>/<item>` across 36 calendar months (Jan 2022 – Dec 2024).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Survey item taxonomy: 32 discrete food items per month (7 white, 15 green, 10 yellow, including "Other" and "Option 6"), spanning 35 columns per month $\times 36 = 1,260$ columns.
  - Microdata ceiling reality: In the microdata, **98.4% to 99.9% of households reported consuming items from all three colour groups in every single month from Jan 2022 to Dec 2024**. Average monthly items consumed rose slightly from 16.4 (Jan 2022) to 17.7 (Dec 2024).
  - Purge of contradictory claims: Prior assertions that "3-colour compliance rose from 14.2% to 74.8%" and that "items rose from 4.8 to 11.6" directly contradict the microdata and are purged.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Dynamic panel GMM (Arellano-Bond) on Household Dietary Diversity Scores (HDDS) is unviable. The survey collected binary monthly consumption recall in a single sitting, not 24-hour food frequency recalls with standardized food-group weights. High reported prevalence creates a statistical ceiling that invalidates dynamic panel estimators.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive longitudinal Poisson / Negative Binomial regressions on monthly item counts:
    $$\mathbb{E}[N_{it} \mid \mathbf{X}_{it}] = \exp\left(\alpha + \sum_{m=1}^{12} \mu_m \mathrm{Month}_m + \sum_{y=2023}^{2024} \tau_y \mathrm{Year}_y + \beta_1 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma}\right)$$
    documenting seasonal oscillations while explicitly declaring the recall ceiling caveat.

---

### Research Question RQ-15: Seasonal Fluctuation in Green Leafy Vegetable Consumption: Evaluating the Summer Peak vs. Winter Trough

- **Core Research Question:** What is the empirical seasonality of green leafy vegetable consumption across calendar months, and does the microdata support the existence of a pre-Kharif "lean hunger gap"?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: Green food consumption columns across May, June, July, and December–February (2022, 2023, 2024).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Inversion of the "Summer Hunger Gap": The microdata directly refutes the claim of a summer green vegetable collapse. Across 2022–2024, **green vegetable consumption peaks during the pre-monsoon summer months (May–July)**, with mean green item prevalence reaching **67.2% in June 2022** and **69.4% in June 2024**.
  - The true seasonal trough occurs in **winter (December–February)**, where green item prevalence drops to **33.2%–42.1%**.
  - Nutrition garden data absence: No nutrition garden variable exists in the survey (the figure "1,328 gardens" in prior drafts was an artifact representing the total survey sample size).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Feasibility: High-dimensional fixed-effects models testing the elimination of a summer hunger gap are rejected because no summer gap existed in the data.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Harmonic seasonal decomposition of green vegetable consumption shares:
    $$S_{it}^{\mathrm{green}} = \alpha + \beta_1 \sin\left(\frac{2\pi m_t}{12}\right) + \beta_2 \cos\left(\frac{2\pi m_t}{12}\right) + \tau_y + \varepsilon_{it}$$
    formally tracing annual cyclicality and winter nutritional vulnerability.

---

### Research Question RQ-16: Legume Crop Expansion and Household Pulse Consumption: Reconciling FDB Microdata and External Midline Benchmarks

- **Core Research Question:** What is the relationship between household cultivation of legume crops and reported monthly pulse consumption, and how can microdata consumption shares be reconciled with external project evaluation indicators?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `What are yellow food did you consumed in.../Chickpea`, `.../Lentil`, `.../Pigeon Pea`, `.../Arhar`, FDB legume crop variables.
  - Secondary Files: `Midline_FASAL_Jaykar (1).docx` (p. 22: +11.27% pulse consumption), `Family Development Booklet-DEHAT.pdf` (p. 8: Kruti Dev food source checkboxes).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Pulse consumption trends: In FDB microdata, monthly pulse prevalence rose across recall years: chickpea (*chana*) from 56.9% to 72.5% (+15.6 pp); lentil (*masoor*) from 81.7% to 94.0% (+12.3 pp); pigeon pea (*arhar*) from 43.3% to 49.0% (+5.7 pp).
  - Provenance of the +11.27% figure: The "+11.27% pulse increase" cited in project reports originates from the external midline report (`Midline_FASAL_Jaykar.docx`), which calculated a 5-item composite that included cowpea (a green-list item). Summing the 4 yellow pulses directly yields an increase of **+8.61 pp (+12.61% relative growth)**.
  - Un-digitised food source field: The physical paper survey booklet contained checkboxes to indicate whether each food was home-grown or market-purchased, but this field was **never digitised into KoboToolbox or CSV exports**.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding: Blinder-Oaxaca decomposition and Seemingly Unrelated Fractional Probit models are unviable due to lack of demographic covariates and missing home-production linkages.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Fractional logit regression of pulse consumption shares against household operational land and legume crop dummy:
    $$\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{PulseShare}_{it} \mid \mathbf{X}_{it}] = \Lambda(\alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{GrowLegume}_{it} + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \tau_t + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma})$$
  - Data Requirement: Digitising the physical booklet checkboxes is required to evaluate actual home-production retention versus market dependency.

---

### Research Question RQ-17: On-Farm Crop Retention for Home Consumption: Structural Determinants and External Data Requirements for Estimating Market Decoupling

- **Core Research Question:** What proportion of gross crop output is retained for domestic household consumption, and what survey modules are required to test whether crop diversification decouples smallholders from retail food markets?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Income received by the farmer from keeping crop for consumption- Paddy in 2022-2024`, `...wheat in 2022-2024`, `...potato in 2022-2024`, `...mustard in 2022-2024`, `...okra in 2022-2024`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Microdata crop retention: The survey records estimated imputed values of retained crops: in 2024, retained cereal/crop values averaged ₹14,200 per household.
  - Survey limitations: The survey contains **zero data on household food expenditures, retail food purchases, or crop-specific physical harvest areas (CDI)**. Prior claims that "home retention equaled ₹1.84 Crore" or that "families saved ₹7,200 annually on retail markets" are ungrounded.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Instrumental Variable Tobit (IV-Tobit) of market purchase displacement is impossible because food purchase expenditures were never collected.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive fractional regression of crop retention shares:
    $$\frac{R_i^{\mathrm{retention}}}{Y_i^{\mathrm{gross}}} = \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{CropCount}_i + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i$$
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Proving market decoupling requires administering a comprehensive household consumption expenditure module (HCES) measuring weekly physical quantities and values of purchased versus self-produced food items.

---

### Research Question RQ-18: Adolescent and Child Collective Participation: Comparative Dietary Profiles of Bal Sansad Member Households

- **Core Research Question:** Do households with children participating in grassroots child collectives (Bal Sansad) exhibit significantly different dietary patterns than non-participating households?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `family_member_repeat` (Repeat roster), `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Is this member part of Bal Sansad?`, `_submission__uuid`, `_uuid`, Monthly White, Green, and Yellow food consumption columns.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Roster sample size: Across the 5,796 individual roster entries, exactly **50 members across 40 unique households** are flagged as active in Bal Sansad (`Yes`).
  - Absence of demographic fields: The roster contains **no age, sex, or schooling variables**. Claims that members are "children aged 6–14" are supported by program design documents but unverified in microdata.
  - Dietary comparison: In the microdata, Bal Sansad households report 97.5% milk consumption versus 96.1% in non-Bal Sansad households (+1.4 pp difference, not +18.4%); summer green vegetable consumption was 84.2% versus 78.5% (+5.7 pp, not +24.1%).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Recursive bivariate probit with instrumental variables is eliminated due to lack of demographic covariates and valid exclusion restrictions.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Cross-sectional propensity-score weighted comparison across the 40 Bal Sansad households versus 1,289 non-participating households:
    $$Y_i^{\mathrm{diet}} = \alpha + \tau \mathrm{BalSansadHH}_i + \beta_1 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \beta_2 \mathrm{CropCount}_i + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i$$
    interpreted as descriptive associational profiling without causal claims.

---

### Research Question RQ-19: Social Protection and Entitlement Buffering: Welfare Analysis of Widow Pension Recipient Households

- **Core Research Question:** How do welfare and dietary indicators compare between households receiving statutory Widow Pensions and the general smallholder sample, and how can female-headed farm dynamics be examined given survey demographic constraints?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, Raw Kobo Export.
    - Variables: `Did you Receive Widow Pension in First six Months` (through Sixth six Months), `Is Leading done for Widow Pension...`, Monthly Food Tracking columns, Net Farm Income 2022–2024.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (p. 5, Section `A. General Information / Social and Demographic Profile`).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline demographic profile: Baseline Report Section A (p. 5) documents that *"Widows make up 10% of total respondents... 1% of respondents are unmarried"*. Note: this refers to respondents, not female household heads.
  - Total absence of gender/marital variables in FDB: The Kobo household survey records **no gender, marital status, or headship variables**.
  - Widow Pension recipients: Widow pension receipt was reported by 36 to 65 households per recall half-year, with **126 unique households** ever reporting receipt.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding: Panel Quantile Fixed Effects regression on female-headed farms is impossible because female headship was never measured. Pension receipt is an imperfect proxy that conflates administrative success with household vulnerability.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive comparative profiling of Widow Pension recipient households ($N=126$) versus non-recipients ($N=1,203$):
    $$Y_i = \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{EverWidowPension}_i + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i$$
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Formal econometric analysis of female headship, intra-household bargaining, and agricultural productivity requires collecting demographic rosters recording member age, sex, marital status, and primary farm decision-maker identity.

---

## Pillar 4: Institutional Collectivization, Gender Agency & Public Leverage

```
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| PILLAR 4 OVERVIEW: INSTITUTIONAL COLLECTIVIZATION, GENDER AGENCY & PUBLIC LEVERAGE                                    |
| Scope: Federation governance, Gram Panchayat Development Plans (GPDP), collective leadership (*Jiska Mudda*), forest   |
| conflict (*Begari/Tihai*), female leadership trajectories, adolescent collectives, and public asset equity.           |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

### Research Question RQ-20: Decentralized Participatory Governance: Qualitative Process Tracing of GPDP Micro-Planning and Scheme Sanction Gaps

- **Core Research Question:** How did the three-tier federation structure of Ajeevika Adhikar Sangathan (AAS) facilitate citizen micro-planning in Gram Panchayat Development Plans (GPDP), and what administrative bottlenecks explain gaps between citizen charter submissions and formal departmental budget sanctions?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `Grant Report January to December 2024.pdf` (pp. 2–3: 1,712 citizens across 5 GPs), `Updated_Fasal Crop wise details_Update _2025.xlsx` (Sheet `AAS INFO`: 82 groups, 1,358 members).
  - Secondary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, 22 Unique Monthly Review Slide Decks.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Federation structure: The `AAS INFO` roster documents **82 grassroots groups comprising 1,358 active members** across 17 villages (not 83 groups and 2,716 members as reported in flawed drafts).
  - GPDP micro-planning: Grant Report 2024 documents that 1,712 community members participated in PRA micro-planning to submit citizen charters to Gram Pradhans in 5 Gram Panchayats.
  - Outcome absence in microdata: Official sanction status, departmental approvals, and financial disbursements resulting specifically from GPDP submissions are **not tracked in the public leverage workbooks or survey files**.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Hierarchical Generalized Linear Models (HGLM / Multi-Level Logit) cannot be estimated because the dependent variable (GPDP proposal approval / funding status) does not exist in workspace data.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Institutional process tracing and qualitative comparative analysis evaluating procedural fidelity, community participation rates, and institutional friction points across the 5 target Gram Panchayats.
  - Data Requirement: Administrative tracking registers recording individual GPDP proposal line items and their corresponding Block/District budget sanction orders.

---

### Research Question RQ-21: The Doctrine of *"Jiska Mudda Uski Agwai"*: Analysis of Co-Recorded Citizen Facilitation and Statutory Entitlement Access

- **Core Research Question:** What is the statistical association between citizen self-facilitation (*leading*) and entitlement receipt across statutory government schemes, and how do survey co-recording mechanics affect causal interpretations of grassroots advocacy?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: 77 scheme blocks $\times 3$ variables (`Where did awareness come from...`, `Is Leading done for...`, `Did you Receive...`), covering PMAY, MGNREGA, Pensions, Ration Cards, Kisan Credit Cards, and Soil Health Cards.
  - Secondary Files: Three Annual Public Leverage Registers (2023, 2024, 2025: ₹7.71 Crore verified total across 577 genuine projects).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Statistical co-recording separation: In the survey microdata, **93% to 98% of all affirmative scheme receipts have the corresponding "Is Leading done" flag marked Yes**. Conversely, among households that did not lead, receipt probability ranges from 0.003 to 0.08.
  - Single-interview artifact: Because awareness, leading, and receipt were collected retrospectively in a single interview sitting, enumerators almost universally checked "Yes" for leading whenever a respondent confirmed receipt.
  - Verified public leverage: Community mobilization contributed to securing ₹7.71 Crore (₹7,70,98,360) in public resources across 577 projects over three years (2023: ₹2.63 Cr; 2024: ₹2.30 Cr; 2025: ₹2.78 Cr).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Structural Equation Modeling (SEM) and Causal Mediation Analysis (Average Causal Mediation Effect / ACME) are statistically invalid because the near-complete co-recording creates severe collinearity and quasi-complete separation.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive cross-scheme correlation matrices and bivariate joint probability profiling:
    $$\Pr(\mathrm{Receipt}_{is} = 1 \mid \mathrm{Led}_{is} = 1) \quad \text{vs.} \quad \Pr(\mathrm{Receipt}_{is} = 1 \mid \mathrm{Led}_{is} = 0)$$
    documenting the institutional pairing of collective advocacy and scheme delivery.

---

### Research Question RQ-22: Agrarian Conflict and Forest Rights Advocacy: Archival Event Study of Begari and Tihai Extortion Dismantling

- **Core Research Question:** What was the sequence of institutional advocacy strategies mobilized by AAS to challenge informal forest department extortion (*Begari* and *Tihai*), and what qualitative evidence exists regarding administrative accountability in the Katarniaghat buffer zone?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `HY4 Report-Remaining Aspects.pdf` (pp. 1–3: Testimonials and administrative petitions), `DEHAT.md` / `DEHAT.pdf` (Founder legacy and institutional history).
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 7–9, Section B).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Historical extortion forms: Project records document customary exploitation by lower-level forest staff: *Begari* (uncompensated forced labor during timber harvesting and road maintenance) and *Tihai* (compulsory extortion of one-third or 10 kg/bigha of crop harvest from forest-fringe plots).
  - Collective mobilization: AAS mobilized affected Tharu and marginal smallholders to file administrative complaints with the District Magistrate and Commissioner, citing the SC/ST (Prevention of Atrocities) Act.
  - Documented outcomes: Qualitative dossiers document the transfer of predatory forest guards and the public affirmation of community cultivation rights. Note: individual badge numbers, transfer order dates, and quantitative crime registries were not compiled into structured datasets.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model (PRIMARY RECOVERABLE SPECIFICATION):**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Synthetic Control Methods (SCM) are eliminated because there are no longitudinal time-series data on extortion incidents across treated and synthetic control villages.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Qualitative event-study and structured institutional process tracing using archival materials (`HY4 Report` and `DEHAT.md`), mapping the temporal sequence of community mobilization, administrative escalations, and retaliatory dynamics.
  - Data Requirement: Archival retrieval of formal First Information Reports (FIRs), Tehsil Day grievance petitions, and District administrative transfer orders.

---

### Research Question RQ-23: Grassroots Female Leadership Trajectories: Comparative Feminist Life Histories and Leadership Emergence

- **Core Research Question:** How do marginal rural women navigate patriarchal, tenurial, and bureaucratic barriers to emerge as grassroots collective leaders within AAS?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: Four Primary Case Study Transcripts (`FASAL_2025/Case stories/` and Grant Reports: Manju, Shanti Devi, Rajkumari Devi, Anarkali).
  - Secondary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`, `FASAL Training data.xlsx`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Primary case evidence: Verified case archives document transformative personal trajectories:
    - **Manju** (Mihinpurwa/Bhattha Bargadaha): Mobilized community action to establish a local school, saving village children from perilous long walks on forest roads; took personal risks planting sugarcane on flood-prone *khala* land.
    - **Shanti Devi** (Bajpur Bankati): Overcame severe domestic abandonment and social stigma; achieved functional literacy and mobile messaging through peer mentorship; emerged as a prominent AAS leader.
    - **Rajkumari Devi**: Transitioned from costly chemical farming to biological turmeric cultivation; achieved over ₹60,000/acre; awarded a certificate of appreciation (*Prashasti Patra*) by the District Magistrate and MLA.
    - **Anarkali** (Tribal leader): Led collective administrative petitions at Tehsil Day against corrupt ration distribution, resulting in official inquiry and dealer replacement.
  - Purge of fictional embellishments: Unverified sensationalism (e.g., "border child predators", false "debt bondage", invented POS scale cross-examinations) is eliminated.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Crisp-set Qualitative Comparative Analysis (csQCA) is mathematically unfeasible. All four case study subjects achieved leadership success ($Y=1$ across all cases). Testing necessity and sufficiency under Boolean minimization requires negative counterfactual cases ($Y=0$).
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Comparative feminist life-history analysis and thematic process tracing, analyzing structural barriers, critical consciousness emergence (*conscientização*), and collective leadership consolidation.

---

### Research Question RQ-24: Youth and Adolescent Collective Action: Institutional Linkages and Social Capital Development

- **Core Research Question:** How do adolescent and youth collectives (Kishor Sangathan and Yuva Sansad) interface with adult agrarian governance in AAS, and what are their institutional contributions to community resilience?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `family_member_repeat` (Repeat roster).
    - Variables: `Is this member part of Kishor Sangathan?`, `Is this member part of Yuva Sansad?`, `_submission__uuid`.
  - Secondary Files: `Grant Report January to December 2024.pdf`, `FASAL 2022/Narrative Report HY2.pdf`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Youth collective microdata: In the repeat roster, **7 members** are flagged as active in Kishor Sangathan and **10 members** in Yuva Sansad across 17 distinct households.
  - Organizational boundaries: Youth collectives operate primarily under DEHAT's parallel child rights and youth empowerment portfolio (funded by Dasra and STF), interfacing with FASAL through shared village geography.
  - Outcomes: The microdata does not record individual educational attainment, marriage age, or anti-trafficking interventions.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Bivariate probit models with endogenous treatment are eliminated due to extremely small sample sizes ($N=7$ and $N=10$) and complete absence of individual outcome metrics.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive sociological analysis of youth participant households, mapping cross-generational participation in AAS and agrarian decision-making.

---

### Research Question RQ-25: Institutional Resilience and Programmatic Adaptation Under Multi-Donor Funding Shocks

- **Core Research Question:** How did DEHAT adapt its institutional architecture and grassroots community mobilizations following external funding contractions in parallel programs during 2024–2025?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT.md` / `DEHAT.pdf`, `Grant Report January to December 2025.pdf`, Three Annual Public Leverage Registers (2023–2025).
  - Secondary Files: Monthly Review Slide Decks (Jan 2025, Jun 2025).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Institutional funding shock context: In mid-2025, the withdrawal of international donors Caritas (€90,195, *Baal Prahri*) and She's the First (₹66 Lakh, *Panchi*) created an organizational deficit of approximately ₹52 Lakh in DEHAT's human rights/child protection portfolio.
  - Programmatic independence of FASAL: FASAL's budget remained stable under APPI (Grant G-2110-10970: ₹2.16 Crore approved, ~₹1.79 Crore disbursed). The funding shock led to staff retrenchments in 12 non-FASAL border Gram Panchayats, but FASAL's 6 core GPs remained fully operational.
  - Grassroots sustainability: Public scheme leverage Mobilized by AAS remained resilient: ₹2.63 Crore (2023), ₹2.30 Crore (2024), and **₹2.78 Crore (2025)** (prior claim of ₹5.56 Cr was an artifact of double-counting total rows).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Interrupted Time Series (ITS) segmented regression and Cox proportional hazards models are unviable because longitudinal financial and activity time series do not exist at monthly frequencies.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Organizational case study and financial resilience analysis examining diversification of funding, community asset ownership, and grassroots federation autonomy.

---

### Research Question RQ-26: Spatial Distribution and Equity of Public Infrastructure Convergence: PMAY Housing and Village Connectivity

- **Core Research Question:** What was the spatial and socio-economic distribution of public infrastructure assets (Pradhan Mantri Awas Yojana pucca homes and village roads) mobilized through AAS advocacy across target Gram Panchayats?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: Multi-Year Leverage Registers (`Levrage January to December 2023.xlsx`, `...2024.xlsx`, `1. Leverage AASs 2025.xlsx`).
    - Variables: `Name of Scheme`, `Physical (unit)`, `Budget/Financail (In Rs)`, `Gram Panchayat`, `Village Name`.
  - Secondary Files: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv` (Variable: `Did you Receive PM Awas in First...Sixth six Months`).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Verified PMAY housing leverage: Across the three annual registers, exactly **280 PMAY housing units** were mobilized (151 in 2023, 66 in 2024, 63 in 2025), representing ₹3.36 Crore in direct public housing capital (valued at the standard normative rate of ₹1,20,000 per unit).
  - Road connectivity: The 2025 register logs village road construction valued at **₹58,04,960** (not ₹4.40 Crore as claimed in flawed drafts). Total road convergence across all registers is approximately ₹1.35 Crore.
  - Microdata linkage: In the FDB survey, 182 households report receiving PM Awas across recall periods. The registers record no caste or gender breakdowns; landholdings can be analyzed by linking beneficiary names to FDB records.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Spatial Lag Models (SAR) and Kakwani concentration index decompositions cannot be executed because geographic shapefiles, GIS coordinates, and household consumption rankings do not exist in the registers.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Cross-village Gini and Herfindahl concentration analysis of infrastructure allocations:
    $$\mathrm{Share}_{j}^{\mathrm{PMAY}} = \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{PopulationShare}_j + \beta_2 \mathrm{MarginalFarmerShare}_j + \varepsilon_j$$
    supplemented by deterministic name-matching to FDB landholdings to evaluate asset targeting.

```
| Scope: Citizen Service Centre (Jan Suvidha Kendra / CSC) service demand, digital frictions in PM-KISAN, subsidized     |
| borewell applications, anti-corruption dynamics, CM Helpline (1076) grievance resolution, and training MIS dividends. |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

### Research Question RQ-27: Citizen Service Centre (CSC / Jan Suvidha Kendra) Transaction Portfolio: Service Demand Composition and Administrative Funnel Bottlenecks

- **Core Research Question:** What was the volume and distribution of citizen entitlement applications processed by the Jan Suvidha Kendra (JSK), and what administrative bottlenecks characterize village-level digital service delivery?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `Jan Suvidha kendra Data 2022,23,24 -25.xlsx` (Sheets: `Sheet1`, `boring avedan 2024`, `2025`, `Sheet2`).
    - Variables: `Applicant Name`, `Father Name`, `Village`, `Scheme`, `Registration Number`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Verified transaction volume: The JSK workbook contains **1,102 genuine citizen service transactions** across four sheets (prior claim of 1,118 included title rows and repeated headers).
  - Scheme breakdown:
    - Individual household toilets (*Tollet*): **394 records** in `Sheet1` (March 2022 – November 2023).
    - Subsidized engine borewells (*boring*): **352 applications** in `boring avedan 2024`.
    - PM-KISAN registrations and updates: **140 records** in sheet `2025`.
    - Departmental seed/horticulture registrations: **122 records** in `Sheet2`.
    - Caste/Income/Birth certificates: **66 records**.
    - Miscellaneous entitlements: **28 records**.
  - Missing duration dates: The workbook does not track application submission dates, departmental processing dates, sanction order numbers, or rejection reasons.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Competing risks duration analysis (Fine-Gray subdistribution hazard) is impossible because transaction processing durations ($T_{\mathrm{sanction}} - T_{\mathrm{application}}$) were never recorded.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Cross-sectional service demand composition analysis and multinomial logistic profiling of service categories across villages:
    $$\Pr(S_i = k) = \frac{\exp(\mathbf{X}_i'\boldsymbol{\beta}_k)}{\sum_{m=1}^{K} \exp(\mathbf{X}_i'\boldsymbol{\beta}_m)}$$
    identifying village-level specialization in sanitation versus agricultural infrastructure.
  - Data Requirement: Access to UP e-District and state portal backend logs to track actual application life cycles and disposal times.

---

### Research Question RQ-28: Digital Frictions and Biometric Exclusion in Central Welfare Transfers: Evaluating PM-KISAN Remediation Records

- **Core Research Question:** What digital and documentation frictions (e-KYC failures, Aadhaar mismatches, land-record seeding errors) impeded smallholder access to PM-KISAN, and how effective was CSC-level intermediation in resolving administrative exclusions?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `Jan Suvidha kendra Data 2022,23,24 -25.xlsx` (Sheet `2025`), `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Aadhaar Number`, `PM Kisan Registration ID`, `Village`, `Farmer Name`, FDB PM-KISAN receipt flags.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (pp. 7–8, Section `B. Access to rights, entitlement, government schemes and services`).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline saturation: At baseline, only **164 farmers (10.9% of 1,504)** reported receiving PM-KISAN quarterly installments (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, p. 8, Section B).
  - JSK remediation records: Sheet `2025` logs **140 smallholders** seeking JSK assistance for PM-KISAN renewals following state-wide mandatory e-KYC and land-registry seeding drives.
  - Data privacy and format check: Of the 140 records, only **12 entries contain valid 12-digit Aadhaar numbers**; the rest are partial text or registration codes. Aadhaar numbers must be strictly de-identified in all published artifacts.
  - Resolution outcomes unrecorded: The sheet does not record whether installments were successfully credited following CSC intervention.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Fuzzy Regression Discontinuity Design (FRDD) around landholding eligibility thresholds is rejected because: (1) PM-KISAN eligibility has no sharp continuous forcing cutoff in this marginal smallholder population; and (2) resolution outcomes are unrecorded.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive documentation bottleneck categorization (land-record seeding vs. biometric e-KYC) and cross-tabulation of FDB PM-KISAN receipt against landholding size classes.
  - Data Requirement: Formal integration with PFMS (Public Financial Management System) disbursement logs under institutional research ethics protocols.

---

### Research Question RQ-29: Spatial Demand for Subsidized Irrigation: Spatial Clustering of Borewell Tube-Well Applications

- **Core Research Question:** What geographic and agricultural factors determine the spatial distribution of demand for subsidized irrigation tube-wells, and how clustered are borewell applications across forest-fringe hamlets?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `Jan Suvidha kendra Data 2022,23,24 -25.xlsx` (Sheet `boring avedan 2024`).
    - Variables: `Registration ID`, `Applicant Name`, `Father Name`, `Village Name`, `Block`.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (p. 11, Section `C. Agriculture / Source of irrigation`), `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline irrigation deficit: Baseline Report Section C (p. 11) confirms that 65.29% of smallholders relied on expensive diesel engine rental, and 33.98% had no irrigation source.
  - Application volume and concentration: Exactly **352 applications** were compiled in 2024. Applications are concentrated in three Gram Panchayats: **Karikot (118, 33.5%)**, **Chahalwa (102, 29.0%)**, and **Badkhadiya (58, 16.5%)**, representing 79.0% of all applications.
  - Application status: The register logs initial application submission; installation confirmations and functioning statuses are absent.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Spatial Poisson and Spatial Autoregressive (SAR) hedonic water cost models cannot be estimated because geographic coordinates, installation dates, and diesel rental prices (e.g. ₹150–250/hr claims) are not recorded in workspace files.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Village-level aggregate demand regression modeling application counts as a function of village smallholder population and baseline irrigation deficits:
    $$\ln(\mathrm{Applications}_j) = \alpha + \beta_1 \ln(\mathrm{Holders}_j) + \beta_2 \mathrm{BaselineDeficitRate}_j + \varepsilon_j$$
  - External Data Dependency: GPS mapping of physical tube-well boring locations and hydro-geological water table depth measurements.

---

### Research Question RQ-30: Intermediation Mechanics in Welfare Delivery: Institutional Alternatives to Informal Brokerage

- **Core Research Question:** How did community-embedded facilitation via JSK and AAS office-bearers alter citizen interactions with local administrative intermediaries (such as Lekhpals and private cyber-café operators)?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: `DEHAT.md` / `DEHAT.pdf` (Section 4: Grassroots governance and corruption challenges), `Jan Suvidha kendra Data 2022,23,24 -25.xlsx`.
  - Secondary Files: Case Studies (Anarkali Devi, Shanti Devi).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Qualitative evidence of rent-seeking: Institutional documents (`DEHAT.md`) record that corrupt intermediaries and local revenue officials (*Lekhpals*) historically demanded informal fees (bribes) ranging from ₹500 to ₹1,500 to certify land possession for PMAY housing and welfare pensions.
  - Data limitations: Specific quantitative bribe amounts, transaction-level extortion logs, and the figure "₹12.5 Lakh in community savings" were never compiled into systematic microdata.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Counterfactual Inverse Probability Weighting (IPW) policy evaluation cannot be estimated because informal fee extractions were not recorded quantitatively across treated and untreated populations.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Qualitative institutional analysis and structured interviews documenting changes in administrative access costs, processing transparency, and collective citizen bargaining.

---

### Research Question RQ-31: Citizen Grievance Redressal and Bureaucratic Responsiveness: Case Evidence from the Chief Minister's Helpline (1076)

- **Core Research Question:** How do rural community collectives utilize centralized digital grievance portals (CM Helpline 1076) to overcome local administrative inertia, and what are the documented resolution outcomes?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: 22 Unique Monthly Review Slide Decks (2024–2026), Case Study Dossiers (Manju).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Slide deck documentation: CM Helpline 1076 is documented in **4 of the 22 unique monthly review presentations**, logging between 2 and 7 specific grievance filings per month.
  - Purge of ungrounded closure rates: Claims of an "average resolution rate of over 80%" or "78.4% resolved within 30 days" are completely unsourced and are purged.
  - Illustrative verified case: The May 2025 presentation (slide 47) documents the collective filing of a 1076 grievance regarding a dysfunctional community handpump, resulting in official inspection and pump re-installation.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Logistic hazard models with unobserved bureaucratic frailty are rejected because the monthly decks do not compile a structured longitudinal grievance database with registration and closure timestamps.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Micro-case process tracing examining specific documented 1076 interventions, analyzing grievance escalation pathways from local Tehsil Days to state-level telephonic monitoring.

---

### Research Question RQ-32: Agronomic Training Exposure and Programmatic Uptake: Descriptive Curricular Analysis of the Capacity-Building MIS

- **Core Research Question:** What was the aggregate volume and thematic composition of smallholder capacity building delivered through FASAL training modules, and how does training exposure correlate with programmatic adoption across Gram Panchayats?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `FASAL Training data.xlsx` (Sheet `Training `).
    - Variables: `Training/ Capacity Building/ Interface meetings/ CRP Meetings (Title)`, `Target Group`, `Date`, `Total Participants`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Verified training volume: The training MIS records **210 validated sessions** and **13,510 cumulative participant attendances** (prior synthesis reported 214 sessions and 12,510 attendees). Note: 34 sessions are post-dated to 2026.
  - Curricular distribution: Sessions span LEISA bio-inputs (2,840 attendances), vegetable nursery management (2,110 attendances), organic pest management (598 attendances), gender rights and leadership (1,850 attendances), and soil health management.
  - Absence of participant microdata: The training spreadsheet records only aggregate batch headcounts across 4 columns. It contains **no participant names, household IDs, or village indicators**.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Generalized Propensity Score (GPS) matching for continuous training dosage is unviable because training attendances cannot be linked to individual households in the FDB dataset.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Curricular portfolio analysis and descriptive time-series tracking of training intensity across agricultural seasons.
  - Data Requirement: Digitizing individual attendance rosters containing participant household UUIDs to enable econometric evaluation of training uptake.

---

## Pillar 6: Distress Migration, Rural Labor Dynamics & Remittance Economics

```
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| PILLAR 6 OVERVIEW: DISTRESS MIGRATION, RURAL LABOR DYNAMICS & REMITTANCE ECONOMICS                                    |
| Scope: Seasonal distress migration hazard, multi-period remittance flows, left-behind farm management, occupational    |
| stratification, and social protection safety nets (MGNREGA).                                                           |
+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
```

### Research Question RQ-33: Longitudinal Trajectories of Seasonal Labor Out-Migration: Reconstructing Raw Half-Year Recall Cycles

- **Core Research Question:** What are the empirical trends in male seasonal distress migration across the six half-year recall periods (2022–2024), and does the data support the hypothesis of a sustained decline in labor out-migration?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: Raw Kobo Export (`Family_Development_Book_...xlsx`), `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: Raw columns 487, 1100, 1797, 2410, 3107, 3720 (`Did your Husband Migrated?` across 6 recall cycles), `In which month your Husband Migrated?`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (p. 5, Section A).
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Temporal dislocation in cleaned CSV: Cleaned CSV files preserved only the last migration block (Jul–Dec 2024, N=213 migrant husbands) and erroneously labeled it as the initial period (SYS-1).
  - True raw longitudinal series: Reconstructing the six raw recall blocks reveals:
    - Period 1 (Jan–Jun 2022): **347 migrant husbands** (26.1%).
    - Period 2 (Jul–Dec 2022): **195 migrant husbands** (14.7%).
    - Period 3 (Jan–Jun 2023): **332 migrant husbands** (25.0%).
    - Period 4 (Jul–Dec 2023): **194 migrant husbands** (14.6%).
    - Period 5 (Jan–Jun 2024): **378 migrant husbands** (28.4%).
    - Period 6 (Jul–Dec 2024): **213 migrant husbands** (16.0%).
  - Refutation of migration decline: Seasonal male migration did **not** decline by 38.6%. Comparing identical calendar seasons reveals a modest increase: Jan–Jun migration rose from 347 to 378 (+8.9%), and Jul–Dec migration rose from 195 to 213 (+9.2%). Migration exhibits pronounced semi-annual seasonality (peaking during post-Rabi pre-monsoon construction seasons), rather than a secular downward trend.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Discrete-time hazard survival models are rejected because: (1) migration is circular and recurrent rather than an absorbing event; and (2) migration months were entered as unstandardized free text without calendar years.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Pooled panel logit model across the 6 reconstructed half-year blocks:
    $$\Pr(\mathrm{Migrate}_{it} = 1) = \Lambda\left(\alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{SummerHalfYear}_t + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \sum_{y=2023}^{2024} \tau_y \mathrm{Year}_y + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma}\right)$$
    documenting robust seasonal cycles and landholding wealth buffers.

---

### Research Question RQ-34: Remittance Distributions and Farm Capital Allocation: Cross-Sectional Analysis of Inflows and Investment Constraints

- **Core Research Question:** What is the volume and distribution of remittances sent by circular migrant workers, and what data structures are required to measure the fungibility of remittances for on-farm capital investments?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: Raw Kobo Export, `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Income contribution by your Husband in the family?` (across 6 blocks), `Total cost...-turmeric`, `Total cost...-okra`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Jul–Dec 2024 remittance baseline: In the surviving CSV column (Jul–Dec 2024), **211 households** report husband remittances totaling **₹87,56,000**. While the raw mean is ₹41,498, it is heavily skewed by a single ₹35,00,000 data-entry outlier; the robust median is **₹20,000**, and the trimmed mean (excluding the outlier) is **₹25,029**.
  - Son remittances: 161 households report remittances from 165 migrant sons, totaling **₹43.98 Lakh** (mean ₹27,317).
  - Multi-period raw totals: Half-year remittance aggregates across raw blocks are: Period 1: ₹86.19 L; Period 2: ₹38.75 L; Period 3: ₹98.47 L; Period 4: ₹46.43 L; Period 5: ₹36.19 Cr (driven by an extreme ₹35 Cr outlier in Row 392); Period 6: ₹87.56 L.
  - Survey limitation: The survey contains **no household expenditure modules** tracking how remittances were allocated across consumption, debt repayment, healthcare, or farm asset purchases.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Three-Stage Least Squares (3SLS) budget share systems are unviable because household consumption expenditures and farm capital asset investments were never recorded.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Robust Tobit and semi-parametric median regressions modeling remittance inflows:
    $$\mathrm{Remittance}_i = \max(0, \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{MigrantCount}_i + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i)$$
  - Data Requirement: Administering a dedicated remittance utilization and household asset acquisition module.

---

### Research Question RQ-35: Labor Substitution and Farm Management in Migrant-Sending Households: Empirical Comparisons of Agricultural Margins

- **Core Research Question:** Do smallholder farms in households with circular male labor migrants achieve different per-acre crop net incomes compared to non-migrant households?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: Migration indicators across recall periods, `Total net income...in 2024`, `Total_Land_(Acres)`.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - 2024 microdata comparison: In the 2024 data, 191 migrant households cultivated 142.6 acres, achieving a mean net crop return of **₹1,02,784/acre**, compared to **₹88,074/acre** across 932 non-migrant households (at 0.20 ac/bigha).
  - Inversion of prior assertions: Prior synthesis claimed migrant farms earned ₹88,400 versus ₹91,200 for non-migrants. The microdata shows the opposite: migrant-sending households achieve higher per-acre returns, primarily reflecting smaller operational holding sizes (0.74 ac vs. 0.81 ac, consistent with the inverse size-productivity relationship).
  - Absence of labor metrics: The survey records no variables on female farm labor hours, hired labor expenditure, or mutual labor sharing (*Madad*).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Endogenous Switching Regression with Propensity Score Matching (ESR-PSM) is eliminated due to lack of demographic covariates and valid instrumental variables.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: OLS regression with interaction terms controlling for farm size:
    $$\frac{\Pi_i}{A_i} = \alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{MigrantHH}_i + \beta_2 \ln(A_i) + \beta_3 (\mathrm{MigrantHH}_i \times \ln A_i) + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma} + \varepsilon_i$$
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Investigating the "feminization of agriculture" requires time-use surveys measuring female decision-making autonomy, managerial control, and domestic care burdens.

---

### Research Question RQ-36: Occupational Stratification and Geographic Destinations of Circular Migrants

- **Core Research Question:** What are the primary destination sectors and occupational categories of rural circular migrants, and how do reported remittances vary across occupations?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary Files: Raw Kobo Export, `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `For what work your Husband migrated?`, `In which state your Husband Migrated?`, Remittance contribution columns across the 6 raw blocks.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Occupational distribution: In the Jul–Dec 2024 recall block, 213 migrant husbands report occupations:
    - Manual / Construction Labor: **141 workers (66.2%)**, median remittance ₹18,000.
    - Masons / Skilled Construction (*Rajmistri*): **25 workers (11.7%)**, median remittance ₹25,000.
    - Drivers / Transport: **12 workers (5.6%)**, median remittance **₹20,000** (prior claim of ₹45,000–₹75,000 is an unsupported exaggeration).
    - Hotel / Restaurant Workers: **7 workers (3.3%)**, median remittance ₹15,000.
    - Factory / Industrial Labor: **18 workers (8.5%)**, median remittance ₹22,000.
    - Other / Unspecified: **10 workers (4.7%)**.
  - Destinations: Primary reported destination states are Uttar Pradesh (urban hubs like Lucknow, Kanpur), Uttarakhand, Delhi/NCR, Punjab, and Maharashtra. Migrant ages and education levels are unrecorded.
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Multinomial logit with Polachek-Bourguignon-Fournier selection correction is eliminated because individual wage rates, migrant ages, education, and destination network instruments were never collected.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Descriptive cross-tabulation and ANOVA decomposition of remittance earnings across occupational categories and destination corridors:
    $$\ln(\mathrm{Remittance}_i) = \alpha + \sum_{k} \theta_k \mathrm{Occupation}_{ki} + \sum_{s} \delta_s \mathrm{DestinationState}_{si} + \varepsilon_i$$

---

### Research Question RQ-37: Social Protection Safety Nets and Distress Migration: Examining the Association Between MGNREGA Job Card Access and Seasonal Outflows

- **Core Research Question:** Is household possession of a functional MGNREGA job card associated with reduced probability of circular distress labor migration?
- **Underlying Data Sources & Specific Variables:**
  - Primary File: `DEHAT_Cleaned_Data_with_Acres.csv`.
    - Variables: `Did you Receive MGNREGA in First...Sixth six Months`, `Is Leading done for MGNREGA...`, Migration indicators across 6 recall blocks.
  - Secondary Files: `Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf` (p. 8, Section `B. Access to rights, entitlement...`), Three Leverage Registers.
- **Primary Data Evidence (Observed Baseline/Midline Metrics):**
  - Baseline job card saturation: At baseline, **64.6% of surveyed farmers (972 of 1,504)** possessed MGNREGA job cards (`Baseline Report - Jaykar.pdf`, p. 8, Section B). Prior claims that "over 82% received less than 15 days work" do not appear in the baseline report and are purged.
  - Microdata job card receipt: In FDB microdata, affirmative responses to MGNREGA receipt range from 900 to 965 households per half-year.
  - Crucial construct mismatch: The survey question asks whether the household *received* the scheme (job card possession), **not the actual number of days worked or wage earnings received**. The public leverage registers record only 1 line item for MGNREGA (an unadjudicated wage complaint of ₹1.21 Lakh).
- **Empirical Methodology & Econometric Model:**
  - Methodological Grounding & Estimator Elimination: Panel bivariate probit with instrumental variables is eliminated because: (1) job card possession does not measure actual public employment days; and (2) AAS leading flags fail exclusion restrictions.
  - Feasible Econometric Specification: Correlational panel logit analyzing the association between reported MGNREGA access and seasonal migration:
    $$\Pr(\mathrm{Migrate}_{it} = 1) = \Lambda(\alpha + \beta_1 \mathrm{MGNREGA}_{it} + \beta_2 \ln(\mathrm{Land}_i) + \tau_t + \mathbf{V}_j'\boldsymbol{\gamma})$$
  - Explicit External Data Dependency: Testing whether public works buffer distress migration requires extracting household-level muster roll records from the national MGNREGA MIS (`nrega.nic.in`), measuring exact persondays worked and wage payment timing.

```
| Scope: PRIMARY RECOVERABLE SUITE (RQ-38 to RQ-43). XLSForm schema collisions, extreme outlier identification, land      |
| unit hygiene, multi-script record linkage, unmerged roster reconciliation, and dual-track productivity standards.     |
```
